# InSilica World V2 — specification

The plan, physics and stage series are in [docs/v2-plan.md](docs/v2-plan.md). This file records what is built: the
code layout, the numerical conventions, the interfaces, every stage's tests with their thresholds, and (§9) the
decision log of agreed changes. v1's SPEC (`../insilica-world/SPEC.md`, decision log §18 #1–#52) stays the reference
for the physics still to be ported.

## 1. Layout

| Path | What |
|---|---|
| `src/insilica/constants.py` | physical constants (from v1's `00-core.js`) |
| `src/insilica/grid/icosa.py` | icosahedral Voronoi mesh with Lloyd/SCVT (port of v1 `buildGrid`, `lloydSCVT`); Hilbert and Morton renumbering |
| `src/insilica/grid/trisk.py` | TRiSK C-grid geometry (port of v1 `getTrisk`), the GPU gather tables, the Perot wind rebuild `cell_winds` |
| `src/insilica/grid/__init__.py` | `Grid`, `get(n, order, lloyd)` with the disk cache `cache/grids/n{n}-{order}-{lloyd}-g{GRID_VERSION}.npz` (#31) |
| `src/insilica/kernels/*.cu` | CUDA C kernels (ASCII only), compiled at run time by NVRTC through CuPy: `layers.cu` (the layered core), `fused.cu` (one-layer TRiSK and FCT transport), `trisk.cu` (the ∇⁴ operator, `mass_update`, `block_sum`) |
| `src/insilica/gpu.py` | kernel loading, `DeviceGrid`, deterministic sums `dsum` |
| `src/insilica/layers.py` | the layered core: stacked layers (Boussinesq or air), time-split RK3 with the fast mode sub-stepped, floor, exchange hook, interfacial stress (§3) |
| `src/insilica/atmosphere.py`, `kernels/air.cu` | the air's column physics for two or three layers: radiation, slab surface and sea ice, sensible heat, evaporation, condensation, convection, heating as θ, the crossings; Held–Suarez forcing (§3) |
| `src/insilica/swe.py`, `transport.py` | v1's one-layer shallow water (kept for V1-ENGINE) and FCT tracer transport (ports of v1 `makeSolver`, `transportStep`), fused kernels |
| `src/insilica/cases.py` | initial states: single-layer benchmarks and the layered cases, with their settings (`PRESETS`) |
| `src/insilica/world.py` | the world: a case on the layered core with tracers; stepping, fields, save/load (§4) |
| `src/insilica/io/save.py` | save-file format (§6) |
| `src/insilica/runcache.py` | spin-up cache `cache/runs/` |
| `src/insilica/server/`, `viewer/` | the engine server (`python -m insilica.server`) and the browser page (§5) |
| `src/insilica/probe.py` | performance probe (`python -m insilica.probe`) |
| `tests/` | pytest suite; `tests/v1ref.py` builds v1 reference data with `tools/v1_dump.mjs` |
| `tools/run_tests.py` | the runner (§7) |
| `cache/` | everything rebuilt on demand (gitignored); `INSILICA_CACHE` moves it |

Environment: Python 3.14 in `.venv`, `pip install -e ".[test]"`. CuPy is `cupy-cuda12x[ctk]`, which brings CUDA 12.9
runtime, NVRTC and headers as pip packages (§9 #6).

## 2. Grid

v1's mesh: an icosahedron with vertices at both poles (cells 0 and 11), midpoint
subdivision n times, Jacobi Lloyd iterations toward a spherical centroidal Voronoi tessellation until no generator
moves 1e-11 rad (poles pinned; v1 stopped at 400 iterations, a rule kept only for the grids compared with v1, #30), corners at circumcenters, neighbors sorted counterclockwise from
east, a fixed 6-slot neighbor table (pad −1), edges listed by lower cell then slot (`eI < eJ`, normal from eI to eJ),
TRiSK vertices numbered by first appearance. Each corner is computed once and shared by its three cells (#19).
Built in v1's numbering, then by default renumbered along a 3-D Hilbert curve of the cell positions for memory
locality (`order='hilbert'`; `'v1'` and `'morton'` are available); the grid keeps `perm` (cell k is v1-order cell
`perm[k]`), and edges and vertices are renumbered by the same rules. From n = 6 the Lloyd iteration runs on the GPU (same formulas; results
differ only by rounding). Arrays keep v1's names (`pos, area, nbr, deg, dist, edgeLen, edgeDir, corners, eI, eJ,
cellEdge, cellSign, ev1, ev2, de, le, xe, ne, kite, areaCell, areaV, circ1, circ2, fV, eoeStart, eoe, w`), plus
`xm`, the Voronoi edge's midpoint (#17): `xe` (where the line between the cell centers crosses the edge) stays the
kites' corner and TRiSK's edge point; `xm` is used by the Perot rebuild `cell_winds` and for sampling analytic
winds onto edges.

GPU gather tables (`trisk.gather_tables`) let each thread sum what v1's loops scatter, in v1's order:
`cellEdgeAsc` (a cell's edges by ascending edge index), `vEdge/vEdgeSign` (a vertex's three edges, ascending),
`vCell/vSlot` (a vertex's three kites by ascending cell).

| n | cells | edges | Lloyd iterations to 1e-11 rad | build (once; cached) | area max/min |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 2,562 | 7,680 | 404 | 3 s | 1.33 |
| 5 | 10,242 | 30,720 | 1,487 | 39 s | 1.41 |
| 6 | 40,962 | 122,880 | 5,412 | 101 s (GPU) | 1.50 |
| 7 | 163,842 | 491,520 | 19,497 | 341 s (GPU) | 1.60 |
| 8 | 655,362 | 1,966,080 | 69,361 | 62 min (GPU) | 1.70 |

The grids compared with v1 (`lloyd='v1'`) stop at v1's 400 iterations, unconverged from n = 5 up as v1's were.

## 3. Numerics on the GPU

- 64-bit throughout; kernels are templated on `REAL` only so the speed probe can time 32-bit.
- NVRTC options `--std=c++17 --fmad=false`: no fused multiply-adds, so products round as in JavaScript and NumPy.
- Every kernel is a gather (one thread per cell, edge or vertex), with no atomics, summing in v1's order. Results are
  bit-identical run to run (D1).
- Layered arrays are layer-major: `x[l * n + i]`, `blockIdx.y` = layer.
- `dsum`: fixed-order tree reduction (256 threads × 8 values per block, shared-memory halving, repeated), the only
  way totals are formed.
- Shallow water (`swe.SWE`): v1's one-layer TRiSK solver per layer: Wicker–Skamarock RK3; v1's positivity limiter
  in every stage (a cell sends at most half its layer per stage); ∇⁴ hyperviscosity from the step's starting wind,
  held through the stages; linear drag. Fused kernels (#18): five per stage (limiter scale, limited flux, cell
  tendency + RK update, vertex PV, edge tendency + RK update), 15 per step (21 with ∇⁴), against V2-0's 27.
- Transport (`transport.Transport`): 3rd-order fluxes f (mean − a − b) + |f| (b − a) β in RK3 stages (a, b the
  scaled curvatures at the edge's two cells; β = 1 is 3rd-order upwind, β = 0 4th-order centered; default 0.25, #20, #21) with Zalesak
  FCT against the upwind solution, on M·psi with the step's mass fluxes; `mass_after` gives the receiving masses in
  flux form, so the tracer total is conserved to rounding. Fused: five cell passes with the edge fluxes recomputed
  where they are summed.
- The fused kernels do v1's arithmetic in v1's order (V1-ENGINE).
- **Layered core** (`layers.Layers`, V2-1): L Boussinesq layers (k = 0 on top), thickness h_k and wind u_k, over
  topography b. Layer k feels the Montgomery potential M_k = g·η + M'_k, with η = b + Σh the free surface and
  M'_k = Σ_{m≤k} g'_{m−1} z_m (z_m the top of layer m, g'_{m−1} the reduced gravity across the interface above it),
  so each layer is v1's TRiSK shallow water with that potential (#24). Momentum also has drag r_k u and a stress
  term (c_k/h_k) u (#29) and ∇⁴ hyperviscosity ν4 = U·d³/π⁴, refused at start-up if ν4·λ²·dt > 1 (λ the vector
  Laplacian's largest eigenvalue, ≈ 27/d²; #25).
- **Time stepping**: time-split RK3 (Wicker & Skamarock 2002, as MPAS-Atmosphere; #28). In each RK3 stage the slow
  tendencies (everything but g·∇η) come from the stage's input state; their thickness-weighted column mean G drives
  the fast mode, which is sub-stepped from tⁿ by forward-backward steps at the true external speed √(gH)
  (τ·√(gH)/d_min ≤ 0.5), with divergence damping γ = 0.1 (the pressure gradient takes η + γ(η − η_prev)). Each layer
  then moves with its own baroclinic flux plus its share of the stage's mean barotropic transport, so the layers
  sum to the sub-stepped surface; η is never a separate variable. **Air** (#55): every gravity wave is sub-stepped,
  external and internal, as MPAS-Atmosphere does: each layer's mass and wind move forward-backward from tⁿ under the
  column's linear stiffness, Φ_k = Σ_{m≥k} S_m P_m (P_m the mass above interface m, S_m = (θ_m − θ_{m+1})·g·dπ/dp
  there, from the step's start; divergence damping γ = 0.1 on every layer), with the stage's full slow tendency held
  fixed; the slow step keeps M_k − Φ_k, the PV flux, friction, π̄∇θ and ∇⁴. Each layer's flux is its stage edge
  thickness times the substeps' mean wind. Slow plus fast is exactly M_k at any state.
- **Floor** (#26): a layer may send out, in any stage, only what it held above the floor (1 m) at the step's start;
  limiting fluxes adds no mass.
- **Exchange** (#27): `Layers.exchange` moves thickness across interfaces with its tracer and momentum (upwind),
  conserving every total; the hook convection, entrainment and overturning will use.
- **Air layers** (V2-2a, #32): h_k is the layer's mass per area (kg/m²) and each layer carries a mean potential
  temperature θ_k (transported like a tracer). Hydrostatics from p = 0 at the top gives M_{L−1} = g·b + θ_{L−1}·π(p_s)
  and M_k = M_{k+1} + (θ_k − θ_{k+1})·π(p_k) (π = c_p (p/p₀)^κ); the pressure force is −∇M_k + π̄_k∇θ_k (π̄ the
  layer's mass-mean Exner function). The fast substeps carry every gravity wave (#55; #37 and #43 were steps toward
  it, and the column's external stiffness Gc now only sizes the number of substeps). Bottom friction is a stress c/h
  integrated exactly over each stage (#36); no interfacial stress (#39 retired by #56: it damped the vertical shear
  storms grow from); the potential-vorticity flux caps a neighbour's thickness at 2× the edge's own at outcrop fronts
  (#38).
- **Air column physics** (`atmosphere.AirPhysics`, #33–#35, #40, #45–#61), for two layers (Held–Suarez) or three
  (the aquaplanet: interfaces θ* = 305 K and θ*₂ = 380 K, #45, #51). Shortwave: v1's (20% absorbed in the air at a
  full column; ocean albedo 0.06, sea ice 0.6), the air's share split between the layers like the vapor part of the
  longwave profile (#59). Longwave: gray, optical depth above p TAU0·[f·s + (1 − f)·s⁴], s = p/p_s, f = 0.5 (Frierson
  et al. 2006's profile: a vapor part and a well-mixed, CO₂-like part that lets the top layer cool; vapor-independent;
  #48, #59); each layer's emissivity is 1 − exp(−its share). A 100 m slab with thermodynamic sea ice (v1's rules: frozen
  below 273.15 K, full cover at 5 cm, a conducting skin over thicker ice), sensible heat 10·(1 + |u|/10) W/m²K and
  evaporation K_EVAP·(1 + |u|/10)·(q_sat(T_skin) − q) into the bottom 100 hPa (#46). Heating changes θ by Q·dt/(μ·π̄).
  Each layer condenses what exceeds its capacity, q_sat averaged over its pressure range (4-point Gauss–Legendre, #50, #62); the water rains out and
  its latent heat counts as heating. Convection (#49): a plume of bottom-layer air, lifted to the interface above and
  condensing on the way, is compared with the air there (at θ*); while buoyant it rises through further interfaces and
  detrains above the last one it beats; the mass that would remove the buoyancy relaxes over τ_conv = 2 h. Air crosses
  each interface only when heated or cooled across it (#47): heating up across a layer's top edge, cooling down across
  its bottom edge, the mass being the layer's mass per kelvin at that edge times |Δθ| — μ/(2|θ* − θ̄|) for the bottom
  and top layers (one known edge), from a truncated exponential between both edges fitted to the mean for the middle
  layer (#58); the bottom and middle layers cross by their mean heating, the top by the heating of its air at its
  bottom edge (#40); a layer wholly beyond an edge crosses to its floor; floor air stays on its own side. Energy and
  water are exact: every re-division of the column books its rounding-level enthalpy residual to the receiving layer.
  Held–Suarez (1994) is the same with Newtonian relaxation of each layer toward the equilibrium θ at its mass-mean
  pressure (the interface's heating from the formula at the interface) and HS friction as the bottom stress
  c = k_f·0.15·μ_ref (also the aquaplanet's surface drag, #57).
- A world tick (layer step + transport + column physics + crossings) allocates nothing and is captured once as a CUDA
  graph, then replayed with one launch per tick (#18); the sun's time is a device scalar advanced inside it.

## 4. The world

`World(n, preset, params, layers, state)`: a case from `cases.PRESETS` on the layered core, with state `h (L,N)`,
`u (L,E)`, `M (L,N)` (layer volume per cell, moved by the same fluxes as h) and `psi (L,N)` (v1 N6's cosine bell
in every layer). A tick is one layer step plus one FCT step with its fluxes. Presets:

| Preset | Layers | Step | What |
|---|---|---|---|
| `w2`, `w5`, `rh4`, `galewsky` | 1 | 450 s · 2^(4−n) (rh4 300 s) | Williamson 2, 5, 6 and the Galewsky jet |
| `phillips` | 2 | 1200 s · 2^(5−n) | a balanced jet at 45°N over a lower layer at rest, 3× the critical shear: baroclinic instability |
| `outcrop` | 2 | 1 h | two ocean layers flowing over a ridge where the lower layer thins to the floor; bottom stress 2.5e-4 m/s |
| `aqua` | 3 air | 900 s · 2^(5−n) | three moist air layers at rest over a slab aquaplanet with sea ice and v1's sun (diurnal and seasonal); θ* = 305 K, θ*₂ = 380 K; surface drag 0.017 kg/m²/s |
| `aqua_dry` | 3 air | 900 s · 2^(5−n) | the same, dry |
| `coupled` | 3 air + 3 ocean | 900 s · 2^(5−n), ocean 1 h | the `aqua` air over the three-layer ocean (#64–#78): wind stress, Kraus–Turner mixing, salinity, sea ice; the air's own surface stress off |
| `basin` | 3 air + 3 ocean | as `coupled` | `coupled` with a thin pole-to-pole wall at 180° that the ocean cannot cross: one ocean basin (#79) |
| `ridge` | 3 air | as `aqua` | `aqua` with v1's T3.6 land ridge at 35–55° in both hemispheres: 2 km within 5° of longitude 0, 1 km out to 15° (#84) |
| `continent` | 3 air + 3 ocean | as `coupled` | `coupled` with one land mass, \|lon\| < 30°, \|lat\| < 70°, 500 m high, open sea at the poles (#84). The outline is only this test world's: land may have any shape |
| `relief` | 3 air + 3 ocean | as `coupled` | `coupled` with V2-4b's land (#85): a continent falling from 1500 m in the east to 150 m at its west coast, a closed basin near 20°N, and polar land north of 68° rising to 1800 m |
| `rce` | 3 air | 1 h | the dry three layers with the annual-mean sun and no dynamics: radiative–convective equilibrium |
| `held_suarez` | 2 air | 900 s · 2^(5−n) | Held & Suarez's forcing from a uniform start; θ* = the area-mean equilibrium θ at 500 hPa (299.6 K); HS friction |

`params` overrides a case's settings: `dt`, `gprime`, `floor`, `drag`, `stress`, `nu4` or `visc4_U`, `rotation`,
`beta`, `div_damp`, `nfast` (fast sub-steps per step; by default 1.2 × what √(gH) of the deepest column needs, then
saved with the world). `layers=K` splits a one-layer case into K equal layers with g' = 0 (they move as one: REPRO).
Fields for the viewer: `h`, `z` (the layer's top), `eta`, `psi`, `speed`, `vort`, per layer; air adds `theta`, `T`,
`Ts`, `ps`, and moist air `Tsurf` (the radiating surface: the ice skin over ice), `q`, `rh`, `rain`, `evap`, `ice`.

## 5. Viewer protocol

`python -m insilica.server [--n 4] [--preset w5] [--port 8765]` serves the page at `http://127.0.0.1:8765/` and a
WebSocket at `/ws` (localhost only). Text messages are JSON; binary frames carry arrays.

| Client → server | Reply |
|---|---|
| `{"type":"hello"}` | `info` (n, N, E, preset, dt, tick, fields, presets), then the grid frame |
| `{"type":"get","field":f,"layer":l,"dtype":"f4"\|"f8"}` | one field frame |
| `{"type":"subscribe","field":f,"layer":l}` | that field now, then after every step or frame |
| `{"type":"play","tpf":k}`, `{"type":"pause"}` | runs k ticks per frame at up to 30 frames/s; `status` |
| `{"type":"step","k":k}` | k ticks, then `status` and the subscribed field |
| `{"type":"new","n":n,"preset":p}` | a new world; `info` and grid to every client |

Errors come back as `{"type":"error","message":…}` without closing the connection. Binary frame: uint32 LE header
length H, H bytes of JSON header, zero padding to a multiple of 8, then the parts, each padded to 8. Field header
`{"kind":"field","name","layer","tick","dtype","count","min","max"}`; grid header `{"kind":"grid","n","N","parts":
[pos f4 3N, corners f4 18N, deg u1 N]}`. Display fields are 32-bit; `"dtype":"f8"` gives the engine's exact bytes.
The page uses three.js r128, vendored in `viewer/vendor/` (MIT licence) so it works offline.

## 6. Save files

An uncompressed zip: `meta.json` (format version 1, kind, n, preset, layers, dt, tick, params, grid generator hash,
engine source hash, array list) and one `.npy` per state array (raw little-endian float64). Entries are written in
sorted order with a fixed timestamp, so the same state always gives the same bytes. Loading rebuilds the grid from n
and refuses a file made with a different grid generator.

## 7. Tests and runner

**Method (agreed 2026-10-26, #22).** Least redundancy and run time, most integrity: one test per link of the
integrity chain (mesh → agreement with v1 → accuracy → conservation → reproducibility → viewer), each as strong as
possible, and nothing a stronger test already implies. A new stage adds tests only for what no existing test can
see, extends an existing test where it can, and retires checks its new tests supersede. Thresholds change only with
the owner's agreement, recorded here.

`python tools/run_tests.py [--perf] [--jobs J] [--cold]` prints the GPU environment, runs the suite in up to 5
processes (pytest-xdist, `--dist loadgroup`; tests sharing a grid share an xdist group) and prints every measured
value against its threshold. `--perf` then runs PERF alone (at every stage close); `--cold` uses an empty cache.

| Test | What it checks | Threshold |
|---|---|---|
| GRID (n = 0–8) | topology (N = 10·4ⁿ+2, E = 30·4ⁿ, V = 20·4ⁿ, 12 pentagons, Euler, every edge in 2 cells and vertex in 3); Σ areas = 4πR² three ways; each cell's kites = its polygon; dual ⟂ primal edges; TRiSK weight antisymmetry; discrete curl(grad) = 0; area max/min; at n = 4 the disk cache returns the freshly built grid | topology exact; ≤ 1e-12; < 1.5 at n = 4; cache bit-identical |
| V1-GRID (n = 2–5) | the mesh built in v1's numbering is v1's mesh: same edges, vertices, neighbor cycles, weight lists, circulation signs; all geometry against v1's dump | same mesh; ≤ 1e-12 · 4^(n−4) (#16) |
| V1-ENGINE | the engine as it runs (fused kernels, CUDA graph, Hilbert-ordered grid) against v1's own results: Williamson 2 at n = 4 (1 and 960 steps of 450 s), a steep mountain with ∇⁴, drag and the positivity limiter active (10 steps), upwind transport of v1's cosine bell (1 and 576 steps) | 1 step ≤ 1e-12; 576–960 steps ≤ 1e-9; limiter exercised |
| ACCURACY | through the layered core: N1 Williamson 2, 5 days at n = 4, 5, 6; W5, 15 days at n = 4; W6 Rossby–Haurwitz, 7 days at n = 4 (v1 N4's phase fit); Galewsky at n = 5 and 6 against an unperturbed twin (v1 N3's metric); Williamson 2 for 30 days at n = 5 (v1 N5); N6 cosine bell at n = 4; Perot rebuild of solid-body rotation at n = 4, 5, 6 | N1 < 1e-3 at n = 4, falls ≥ ×3 per level; W5 energy drift < 1e-4; W6 phase speed within ±10%; Galewsky day 6 > 0.3, growth > ×20, max |u| < 200; 30-day wind error < 1 m/s; N6 ≥ 2× better, min > −1e-9, drift < 1e-12; Perot max ≥ ×1.8, rms ≥ ×3.5 per level |
| WAVES | at n = 5 with long steps (fast mode sub-stepped): an external wave in air (300 m/s, 30-min steps) and ocean (200 m/s, 1-h steps), and a two-layer internal wave against the exact free-surface speed; at n = 6 the ocean's equatorial Kelvin wave (its default fixed-g' layers, friction and smoothing, no air; a first-baroclinic-mode bump) against c1 of the layered eigenproblem (3.073 m/s), tracked along the equator from day 5 to 25 (V2-3) | external within 2%, internal within 3% |
| BAROCLINIC | Phillips two-layer instability at n = 6: a balanced jet at 45°N over a lower layer at rest, at 3× and 0.3× the critical shear 2βL_d², disturbance energy against an unperturbed twin from day 1 to day 20 | ≥ ×100 at 3×; ≤ ×2 at 0.3× |
| CONSERVE (n = 4, 6) | for air (n = 4): the moist column's ledgers every step (absorbed sunlight − OLR = the change in enthalpy, latent energy, slab heat and ice; vapor change = evaporation − rain), air mass, tracer and vapor (against evaporation − rain) through 10 days of the moist three-layer `aqua`, and adiabatic air energy (two layers); and the outcrop case (15 days at n = 4, 1 day at n = 6): each layer's volume, mass and tracer, the thinnest layer; then an exchange of up to 40% of each donor: volume, tracer, momentum and M = h·A; and energy on the subcritical two-layer jet with no dissipation at all | ≤ 1e-13; never below the floor; exchange ≤ 1e-13; energy drift < 1e-4 |
| CONSERVE coupled (n = 4) | `basin` for 2 days after its cached spin-up, checked every step and every ocean step | the ocean's own step keeps its heat, salt, total volume and (outside the mixing) each layer's volume ≤ 1e-13; salt changes by the virtual flux of rain, evaporation and sea ice ≤ 1e-13; the wind stress conserves the air's and the ocean's momentum away from the walls ≤ 1e-13 of the gross; \|energy change − TOA\| ≤ 0.1 W/m² (the air core's own non-conservation, #81) |
| CONSERVE land (n = 4) | `continent` for 2 days after its cached spin-up, every step; a save and reload | column energy and land water ≤ 1e-13; salt against fresh water and runoff ≤ 1e-13; no water across a coast; \|energy change − TOA\| ≤ 0.1 W/m²; the reload continues identically |
| GYRE (n = 5) | the ocean alone in a closed basin (15–55°N, 60° wide) under a prescribed wind stress, 3 years, the last one's mean: full-depth transport at 35°N | interior within 30% of Sverdrup; the west bin northward and > 3× the mean interior bin |
| WATER | without air: a bowl fills to a level surface (n = 4, 3 years); a shallow pulse's speed on a slope (n = 4, 5, 6); seepage to the sea (n = 4, 1 year). One column step of `ridge`: soaking in, frozen ground, a spring (#85) | level ≤ 1 mm; water ≤ 1e-13; speed within 20% of the set 1 m/s at each n; soaking ≤ 1e-9 off the rule; frozen ground takes 0; a spring returns > 0.9 of the excess |
| ICE | without air: a dome grown from nothing on a flat bed (n = 5, 6; 200 000 years) against the exact shallow-ice profile on the sphere; an island's ice cap (n = 4, 20 000 years), open and behind a wall (#86) | height and volume within 10% at n = 5 and 6, height closer at n = 6; ice ≤ 1e-13; some reaches the sea, its fresh-water count ≤ 1e-9; ice ≥ 0; none past a wall; on `relief_ice` (n = 4: `relief` with its land poleward of 68° raised 2 km) a started 2 km polar sheet's surface (its change less what the ice's flow moved) loses ≤ 50 kg/m² a year; growth from nothing reported (#87, #162) |
| REPRO | repeat runs, graph against direct ticks and repeated tree sums; save → load → save bytes and save-at-T + continue against a straight run; spin-up cache miss then hit; a one-layer case split into two equal layers with g' = 0 against itself and the one-layer run; the Hilbert-ordered grid against v1's numbering after 960 steps | bit-identical; numbering ≤ 1e-12 |
| VIEWER | the protocol with a Python client: grid, exact f8 bytes, f4 bytes, step +1/+5, play pushes, pause holds, error reply, page and three.js served | all correct |
| RCE | the `rce` world (three dry layers) at n = 4 for 20 years | steady (< 0.1 K/yr); each layer ≥ 10% of the mass (#53); mean surface 295 ± 5 K (dry, #41); top-of-atmosphere imbalance ≤ 0.5 W/m² |
| HELD-SUAREZ | `held_suarez` at n = 5 for 400 days, zonal means over days 201–400 | kinetic energy days 301–400 within ±20% of 201–300; upper-layer jet 12–25 m/s at 30–50° in both hemispheres (#41); lower-layer westerlies at 40–55°, easterlies at 0–15°; zonal-mean wind beyond 80° below 5 m/s in both layers and hemispheres (#44) |
| CLIMATE | the moist three-layer `aqua` at n = 4, annual means of year SPIN + 1 after a cached spin-up (#54) | mean surface (skin) 288 ± 3 K; \|TOA\| < 1 W/m²; the bottom layer's residual mass transport equatorward at 10–30° in both hemispheres (Hadley cell); bottom-layer zonal wind < 0 at 5–25° (trades) and > 0 at 35–55° (westerlies); rain at 0–10° > 2× and at 40–60° > 0.5× that at 15–30°; global rain 2–3 mm/day. Reported only until the ocean stage (#52): equator − pole surface difference, 60°N seasonal range |
| CLIMATE coupled | `basin` at n = 4, annual means of year 21 after a cached 20-year spin-up (#77, #79) | mean surface 288 ± 3 K; the air's balance \|TOA − the ocean's and ice's heat uptake\| < 1 W/m²; the bottom layer's residual transport equatorward at 10–30° in both hemispheres; trades < 0 at 5–25° and westerlies > 0 at 35–55° (bottom layer); ITCZ (0–10°) > 1.3× and 40–60° > 0.5× the subtropics' rain (15–30°); global rain 2–3 mm/day; equator − pole 30–60 K; 60°N seasonal range > 3 K; ocean heat transport ≥ 0.2 PW poleward at 20° and 30° in both hemispheres (from the currents' heat convergence); equatorial (\|lat\| < 5°) thermocline base deeper in the basin's west third than its east third by > 10 m and SST warmer by > 0.3 K; sea-ice edge (> 1 kg/m²) between 50° and 75° all year; reported only: the equatorial dip (4–8° − 0–2° SST, east third) |
| CLIMATE land | `continent` at n = 4, annual means of year 21 after a cached 20-year spin-up (#84) | mean surface 288 ± 3 K; \|TOA − uptake\| < 1 W/m²; Hadley cell, trades and westerlies as above; ITCZ over the sea > 1.3× its subtropics; 40–60° > 0.5×; global rain 2–3 mm/day; 45–65°N seasons larger over land than sea; reported only: equator − pole, equatorial land rain |
| RIDGE | `ridge` at n = 5, the year after a cached 1-year spin-up: v1's T3.6 and T3.7 | ridge top > 10 K colder than the adjacent sea; reported only (#84): the rain shadow against its target > 2× |
| WEATHER | the moist `aqua` at n = 5, 60 days after a cached 1-year spin-up | B4: eddy / mean-flow kinetic energy at 30–60° over the tropospheric layers (#60) > 0.2; B9 reported against its threshold — mid-latitude (35–55°) daily rain's coefficient of variation > 1 and, on the median day, the wettest 20% of cells near 45° get > 50% of the rain — as a known near miss (#63); B10 reported (wettest tropical row's max/mean, 5-day map correlation) |
| SPINUP | the deep ocean's asynchronous spin-up (#107): `earth` n = 4 a year in, the next year coupled and replayed by the ocean alone | SST rms ≤ 0.5 K; heat uptake within 15%; salt ≤ 1e-13 |
| LONGWAVE | the k-table longwave (#110): a NumPy evaluation of the model's method against line-by-line on seven AFGL columns (three unfitted); the GPU kernels against it on 64 of a world's cloudy sub-columns | OLR, surface down ≤ 1%; heating ≤ 0.2 K/day per sub-level below 1 hPa; 2×CO₂ forcing ≤ 10%; kernels ≤ 1e-4 |
| EARTH | `earth` n = 4 with the k-tables, annual means of year 9 after a cached 8-year spin-up (#112): absorbed sunlight, OLR, clear-sky OLR, surface longwave down, surface sunlight | each within 5% of 240, 239, 266, 342, 161 W/m² (Wild et al. 2013, CERES EBAF); known failure to V2-6b (#112) |
| PERF (`--perf`) | ns per layer-edge per slow step at n = 4–8 for L = 1, 2, 4; launch overhead; cost per model hour of whole world ticks for air (n = 6, `aqua`, with its physics), the coupled world (n = 6, `basin`), land (n = 6, `continent`) and ocean (n = 8, `outcrop`) | n = 8 ≤ 16 ns; air ≤ 40 ms (#61), coupled ≤ 50 ms (#82), land ≤ 50 ms (#84) and ocean ≤ 180 ms per model hour |

## 8. Git

One branch per stage (`stage-v2-0`, …), checkpoints pushed there, merged to `main` when the stage closes.

## 9. Decision log

| # | Date | Area | Decision |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2026-10-26 | Engine | CUDA kernels in 64-bit driven from Python through CuPy; the browser is the viewer (plan §6, §9). |
| 2 | 2026-10-26 | Repository | A new repository next to v1; v1 is paused and read-only (plan §9). |
| 3 | 2026-10-26 | Layer thickness | Set by physics with a minimum-thickness floor; free ocean interface with entrainment; isentropic air interface, hybrid with a terrain-following lower layer; the remap nudge is a fallback, off by default (plan §3). |
| 4 | 2026-10-26 | Fast modes | Split-explicit at true wave speeds (plan §4), not semi-implicit. |
| 5 | 2026-10-26 | Resolution | Showcase air n = 6 + ocean n = 8; tests at n = 4–5; a variable-resolution region stays a later option. |
| 6 | 2026-10-26 | Python and CuPy | Python 3.14 in a project `.venv` (CuPy 14.2 has Python 3.14 wheels). `cupy-cuda12x`, with the `[ctk]` extra: CuPy 14's bundled CCCL headers need CUDA ≥ 12.8, and with the installed 12.6 toolkit CuPy's own reductions (e.g. `cupy.sum`) failed to compile; the pip CUDA 12.9 runtime, NVRTC and headers fixed it. |
| 7 | 2026-10-26 | Tests | pytest with pytest-xdist (≤ 5 processes), a runner that prints measured values against thresholds, and a spin-up cache keyed by a hash of every engine source. |
| 8 | 2026-10-26 | Viewer | JSON control messages and binary frames; display fields 32-bit, exact 64-bit on request; three.js r128 vendored; localhost port 8765. |
| 9 | 2026-10-26 | Porting | v1's grid, TRiSK, solver and transport ported step for step in v1's order, checked against dumps of v1's own results (G4, K1). Kernels compiled with `--fmad=false`. |
| 10 | 2026-10-26 | Lloyd | v1's rule at every n (≤ 400 iterations, tolerance 1e-11); n ≥ 6 on the GPU. |
| 11 | 2026-10-26 | Cell order | v1's numbering in V2-0; a Morton (Z-order) renumbering is measured by P1. |
| 12 | 2026-10-26 | Saves | The zip/npy format of §6; v1 save files are not imported. |
| 13 | 2026-10-26 | Git | One branch per stage, merged to `main` at the stage's close (as v1 §18 #48). |
| 14 | 2026-10-26 | G4 procedure | Edges and vertices are compared through maps matched by their cells (an edge by its cell pair, a vertex by its cell triple), and each cell's slots through the rotation that aligns its neighbor cycle. Where a neighbor lies exactly due west of a cell (angle ±π, on the icosahedron's symmetry lines), rounding picks which neighbor comes first, in v1 and in V2 alike: 3–6 cells per level start their slot cycle at a different neighbor, which renumbers some edges. The mesh itself (cells, edges, vertices, cycles, weight lists, signs) is identical. |
| 15 | 2026-10-26 | Mesh precision | Asked for by the owner before closing V2-0, so that G2 and G4 hold at their 1e-12 thresholds. v1's formulas (circumcenter from (b − a) × (c − a), area from a·(b × c), arc from atan2(|a × b|, a·b)) lose precision on small triangles: against a 40-digit reference with the points on the unit sphere (`tools/precision_check.py`), at n = 8 corners are off by 5.6e-14 rad, areas by 3.0e-12 and arcs by 1.1e-14 (relative). V2 now forms them from chords in a tangent frame at the first point, with radial parts from a·b − 1 = −|b − a|²/2 and arcs as 2 asin(|b − a|/2): 2.5e-16, 6.8e-16 and 2.7e-16. G2's cell-vs-kites check went from 1.4e-12 / 7.3e-12 / 2.7e-11 (n = 6, 7, 8, failing) to 1.7e-14 / 3.2e-14 / 7.2e-14. Against v1, G4 moved from 1.4e-14 / 7.0e-14 / 3.5e-13 / 1.41e-12 to 1.2e-14 / 5.2e-14 / 2.3e-13 / 1.29e-12 (n = 2–5). From v1's own positions at n = 5, v1's cell areas are off by 5.1e-13 and V2's by 4.6e-15, so the remaining G4 gap at n = 5 is v1's own rounding (≈ 1e-12 in the TRiSK weights), plus 8.9e-15 rad of position difference from the unconverged Lloyd iteration. K1 is unchanged (2.3e-15 after 1 step, 4.2e-14 after 960). G4 at n = 5 stays failing until the owner decides (decided in #16). |
| 16 | 2026-10-26 | G4 threshold | Agreed with the owner: G4's geometry bound scales with the grid level as 1e-12 · 4^(n−4), because v1's own small-triangle rounding (#15) grows about 4× per level. The bound stays 1e-12 at n = 4, where v1's tests ran; it is stricter below (6.3e-14 at n = 2, 2.5e-13 at n = 3) and 4e-12 at n = 5. Measured 1.2e-14 / 5.2e-14 / 2.3e-13 / 1.29e-12: all pass. G2 keeps its flat 1e-12, which it now meets at every level. |
| 17 | 2026-10-26 | Edge midpoint | Agreed with the owner: the Perot wind rebuild and the sampling of analytic winds onto edges use the Voronoi edge's midpoint `xm`. With v1's edge point `xe` the Perot error's maximum stayed near 2% at every level (rms halved); with `xm` the maximum falls ×2.1 and the rms ×3.9 per level (E1). `xe` remains the kites' corner and TRiSK's edge point, since the kites are the exact cell–triangle overlaps only with it. N1 moved from 1.99e-4 to 2.01e-4. |
| 18 | 2026-10-26 | Fused kernels, CUDA graph | Agreed with the owner. Kernels fused to 15 launches per RK3 step (from 27) and 5 transport passes (from 11), with bit-identical results; each world tick captured once as a CUDA graph and replayed with one launch. The unfused kernels stay as the test reference (E2). |
| 19 | 2026-10-26 | Cell order | Agreed with the owner: cells renumbered along a 3-D Hilbert curve by default (measured against Morton: equal at n = 8, faster at n = 6–7). Tests that compare with v1 map through `perm`. Each corner is now computed once and shared by its three cells: per-cell copies differed in the last bits, so an edge's length depended on which cell it was read from (E3 read 6e-15 at n = 4 before the change, 2.9e-16 after). |
| 20 | 2026-10-26 | Transport bug from v1 | Found by the renumbering: v1's 3rd-order flux, f (mean − a − b) + \|f\| (f ≥ 0 ? b − a : a − b), takes the curvature of the edge's eI cell for both flux signs, so for f < 0 it is downwind-biased and the result depends on how edges are oriented (FCT3 differed by 6e-3 after one step on the renumbered grid). Corrected to f (mean − a − b) + \|f\| (b − a) β, which uses the upwind cell's curvature and is orientation-independent (E4). β (the upwind weight) is a parameter, default 1 pending the owner's choice; measured FCT3 L2 on the one-revolution bell: β = 1 0.445 / 0.126 (n = 4 / 5), 0.5 0.357 / 0.076, 0.25 (MPAS's default) 0.305 / 0.054, 0 0.263 / 0.063. v1's mixed form gave 0.363 at n = 4; its partly downwind (anti-diffusive) half sharpened the bell. N6 with β = 1: ×2.06 (threshold ≥ 2×). K1 checks v1's form through the reference kernel on v1's numbering. |
| 21 | 2026-10-26 | Transport β | Agreed with the owner: the engine's default upwind weight is β = 0.25, MPAS's default (`transport.BETA`; a world can override it with `params['beta']`). N6 FCT3 L2 0.305, ×3.01 better than upwind (threshold ≥ 2×); at n = 5 the bell's L2 is 0.054 (β = 1: 0.126). E2 compares the fused and reference kernels at this β. |
| 22 | 2026-10-26 | Test method | Agreed with the owner, and to be kept for every later stage: least redundancy and run time with the most integrity (§7). The 45 results of V2-0/V2-0b became seven tests with every threshold kept: GRID = G1 + G2 + G3 + grid cache; V1-GRID = G4; V1-ENGINE = K1, now run on the engine as it runs (fused kernels, CUDA graph, Hilbert order); ACCURACY = N1 + N6 + E1; CONSERVE = C1 (world; the bell's budget is in N6); REPRO = D1 + S1 + spin-up cache + E2's graph check + E4, plus a new check that two identical layers stay bit-identical to each other and to one layer (replacing E2's multi-layer coverage); VIEWER = W1. Retired: F0 (every test needs the GPU; the runner prints the environment), E3 (implied by V1-ENGINE and REPRO's numbering check), E2's fused-against-reference comparison together with the reference kernels (`reference.py` and the one-loop-per-kernel kernels in `trisk.cu`; V1-ENGINE checks the engine against v1 directly), K1's check of v1's old 3rd-order form (no longer run, #20), R1 (the runner's summary line). PERF runs only with `--perf`, at every stage close. Suite 206 s → 9 s warm. |
| 23 | 2026-10-26 | Layer thickness | Agreed with the owner: physics alone controls the layers (heating and cooling across isentropes, convection, entrainment, Ekman pumping, overturning). The remap toward a target thickness (ALE), #3's fallback, is not built; it is to be recommended again only if a phenomenon cannot be made to emerge physically. With two layers per fluid a remap acts as artificial vertical mixing and would hide physics errors. |
| 24 | 2026-10-26 | Layered core | Boussinesq density layers with the Montgomery potential M_k = g·η + M'_k (§3); the potential is one function, so V2-2 can add isentropic air layers. |
| 25 | 2026-10-26 | Hyperviscosity | ν4 = U·d³/π⁴ (`visc4_U`, d the mean cell spacing): one setting serves every level. Measured: the vector Laplacian's largest eigenvalue is ≈ 27/d², not (π/d)², so the grid's shortest modes damp at ≈ 7.5·U/d; a world refuses ν4·λ²·dt > 1 at start-up (the Phillips case first had visc4_U = 50, unstable at 2.13; it uses 20, 0.85–0.98). |
| 26 | 2026-10-26 | Floor | 1 m, enforced by limiting each layer's outflow per stage to what it held above the floor at the step's start (no mass added). |
| 27 | 2026-10-26 | Exchange | A conservative inter-layer exchange of thickness with its tracer and momentum (upwind), for the vertical processes of V2-2 and V2-3. |
| 28 | 2026-10-26 | Fast mode | Agreed with the owner: time-split RK3 (MPAS-Atmosphere style), with MPAS-Ocean's barotropic averaging filter to be added only if the ocean case showed fast-mode noise. It did: a balanced two-layer ocean (n = 6, 1-h steps, 15 fast sub-steps) grew its free-surface error from 2.8e-6 m to 1.2e-2 m in 165 days, against 3.0e-5 m for 60-s steps without sub-stepping; at 15-min steps it blew up by day ~100. The averaging filter inside RK3 stages (sub-stepping over twice the stage interval and averaging, with the averaged transport for the layers too) made every two-layer run blow up within days. Divergence damping γ = 0.1 (Skamarock & Klemp 1992, the standard companion of time-split RK3 in MPAS-Atmosphere and WRF) fixed it: 3.0e-6 → 1.5e-5 m over 180 days at both 1-h and 15-min steps, matching the short-step reference. Agreed with the owner in place of the filter. |
| 29 | 2026-10-26 | Bottom stress | A layer thinning to the floor over a ridge ran away (its wind 16 → 111 m/s in three days, then NaN), with or without ∇⁴ and at shorter steps: a fixed-rate drag cannot hold a nearly massless layer. Bottom friction now acts as a stress, decelerating a layer as c/h (the physical form; c = 2.5e-4 m/s, ocean-typical, on the outcrop case's lower layer): 30 days stable, the thin layer's fastest wind under 4.3 m/s. CONSERVE's energy check moved to a case with no dissipation (the subcritical jet), since the outcrop case now dissipates by design. Agreed with the owner. |
| 30 | 2026-10-26 | Mesh smoothing | ACCURACY N1's convergence (×2.94 from n = 5 to 6, threshold ×3) is limited by the mesh: with v1's rule the Lloyd iteration stops at 400 iterations, unconverged from n = 5 up (area max/min 1.42 / 1.54 / 1.69 / 1.79 at n = 5–8). Iterated to its 1e-11 tolerance, N1 falls ×3.80 then ×3.08 (the n = 6 mesh after 4,000 iterations). Measured cost to the tolerance, once per level (grids are cached): n = 6 5,412 iterations, 106 s; n = 7 19,497, 388 s; n = 8 not reached after 18,000 iterations (979 s, last move 5e-8), about 55,000 (~50 min) projected. Proposal: iterate to the tolerance at every level for the engine's grids, keeping v1's 400-iteration rule only for the grids V1-GRID and V1-ENGINE compare with v1. Agreed with the owner: `grid.get(n, lloyd='converged')` (default, cap 200,000) or `lloyd='v1'`; a renumbered grid starts from the cached v1-numbered one, so Lloyd runs once per level. |

| 34 | 2026-10-26 | Crossing θ* | Physics alone moves air across θ*: mass crossing = μ·|Δθ|/(2|θ* − θ̄|) from heating or cooling across it; a layer wholly beyond θ* crosses to its floor; floor air stays on its side of θ* (its excess heat moves to the other layer, enthalpy for enthalpy). Energy exact. *(Generalized to every interface, #47; the middle layer by #58.)* |
| 35 | 2026-10-26 | Held–Suarez | Two-layer Held & Suarez: relaxation toward the equilibrium θ at each layer's mass-mean pressure with the HS rates averaged over the layer's σ range; HS friction as the lower layer's bottom stress; started as HS's protocol starts (uniform, at rest), since the equilibrium gradient at rest swung the interface by hundreds of hPa. |

| 38 | 2026-10-26 | Thin-layer PV flux | In the potential-vorticity flux a neighbour's thickness counts for at most 2× the edge's own: at outcrop fronts a floor-thin vertex's q times a thick neighbour's flux amplified the Coriolis force on the thin layer 50–80×. Smooth layers never reach the cap. |
| 39 | 2026-10-26 | Interfacial stress | Agreed with the owner (option 1, before any hybrid layer): turbulent momentum exchange between the layers, τ = 0.01·(u_k − u_{k+1}) kg/m²/s, decelerating each layer as τ/h, solved exactly each step (column momentum exact), its kinetic energy returned as heat in proportion to mass. A full layer feels it over ~6 days; a layer at its floor is locked to the one above within minutes. With it, layers thinned to the ground by eddies stay stable. *(Retired by #56.)* |
| 40 | 2026-10-26 | Crossing by the heating at θ* | The stratified upper layer crosses down by the heating of its air at θ*, not its layer-mean heating: in Held–Suarez the polar upper layer (0–500 hPa) is heated on average (its stratosphere) while its air near θ* is cooled, so mass never came down and the lower layer drained (2,400 → 182 kg/m² in 120 days, v1's old failure). With the rule it settles at 21% of the mass. Gray radiation gives only layer means, so the radiative case keeps them. |

RCE is judged against a dry planet, mean surface 295 ± 5 K (v1's 288 ± 3 K was moist, with evaporative cooling). HELD-SUAREZ's upper-jet bound is 12–25 m/s for the layer mean: the upper layer spans ~0–790 hPa, over which HS's 30 m/s peak at 250 hPa averages ~15–20 m/s. θ* is 300 K for the aquaplanet and the HS 500 hPa mean (299.6 K) for Held–Suarez; the rule "half the mass below θ*" put it near 400 K, not Earth-like. |
| 42 | 2026-10-26 | Air speed | Agreed with the owner: PERF's air gate is 30 ms per model hour at n = 6 with the air physics and the approved 15-minute-at-n = 5 step (the 15 ms estimate was for dynamics alone at 12-minute steps). | 43 | 2026-10-27 | Fast mode for air: full external stiffness | Amends #37 (owner asked to trace and fix the summer-pole vortex). The fast mode's coefficient is the column's full external stiffness, the mass-weighted change of the layers' Montgomery potential when every layer's mass changes in proportion: R·T_s/μ plus, for each interface j, (θ_j − θ_j+1)·R·(p_j/p₀)^κ·P_j/μ² (P_j the mass above it). With the bottom term alone the interfaces' share of the external restoring force (~8%) stayed in the slow step, where RK3 cannot follow a wave turning ~3 radians per step: a 2Δt column-mass mode grew at the pentagons once the air was warm (adiabatic core started 30 K warmer: unstable by day 25; cooling or a 900 s step: stable). It made Held–Suarez's spurious polar vortex (10–27 m/s at > 85°, gone at 450 s) and blew up the aquaplanet (n = 4 day ~70 dry, ~230 moist; n = 5 before day 60). With the full stiffness: Held–Suarez's poles 0.1–2 m/s at 900 s, the dry aquaplanet stable at 4 fast sub-steps. Not needed after it (tried and removed): a stricter fast Courant number for air, divergence damping of each layer in the slow step. |

| 65 | 2026-10-27 | Wind stress, exact | Agreed (V2-3 plan): the air's bottom layer (mass per area m_a) and the ocean's mixed layer (m_o = ρ_w h₀) are pulled together by τ = c·(u_air − u_ocean), c = 0.017 kg/m²/s (the slab's surface drag, #57), integrated exactly over each air step: their difference decays as exp(−c(1/m_a + 1/m_o)dt) and their mass-weighted mean, the column's momentum, is unchanged (CONSERVE coupled: 1.5e-17). The kinetic energy removed, ½ m_a m_o/(m_a + m_o)(Δu₀² − Δu₁²), warms the air like the interfacial stress's (#39), and τ² is summed for the mixed layer's u*. The air's own surface stress is off in the coupled presets. At a wall (#79) the ocean is held fixed (#81). |
| 66 | 2026-10-27 | Convective adjustment | Agreed (V2-3 plan: overturning with the Kraus–Turner mixing): wherever a layer is denser than the one below, the column's blocks mix completely, heat (and salt, #76) conserved and interfaces unmoved, before every ocean step and in the daily mixing. Left to the daily mixing alone, the two-layer ocean's stratification inverted between mixings and the interface grew without bound (the layered model's Rayleigh–Taylor instability). With fixed g' (#64) convection moves heat and salt, not water. |
| 67 | 2026-10-27 | Daily mixing | Agreed: the Kraus–Turner entrainment and shoaling act once a day (mix_dt) on the day's mean surface fluxes and rms wind stress. Mixed hourly, each day's sunshine shoaled the layer at once to its Monin–Obukhov depth and folded its warm water into the layer below, and the night's slow entrainment never won it back (the bulk two-layer model's known diurnal pathology). Constants m = 0.5, n = 0.2 (Gaspar 1988); m 1.25 was tried and barely changed the two-layer climate. |
| 68 | 2026-10-27 | Mixed-layer limits | Agreed: the mixed layer entrains only across a jump of more than dTmin = 0.1 K (as buoyancy g·2e-4/K·dTmin); below it the layers are already mixed by the adjustment and entraining would only move thickness. It stays between hmin = 10 m (every ocean layer's floor) and hmax = 1,000 m (Earth's deepest winter mixed layers). |
| 69 | 2026-10-27 | Ocean stresses | Agreed: the ocean's layers exchange momentum by an interfacial stress c = 5e-4 m/s (C_d|Δu| ~ 1e-3 × 0.5 m/s; exact pairwise decay as #39), and the abyss feels a bottom stress 2.5e-4 m/s (the outcrop case's). Without the interfacial stress, a mixed layer thinned to its floor by equatorial upwelling ran away toward the wind's speed. The ocean's ∇⁴ viscosity uses U = 0.5 m/s. |
| 70 | 2026-10-27 | Sunlight below the mixed layer | Agreed with the owner: of the sunlight entering the water, the part passing depth z, f(z) = 0.58 e^(−z/0.35 m) + 0.42 e^(−z/23 m) (Paulson & Simpson 1977, clear water), heats the layers below, each the part absorbed within it, every air step. Without it the two-layer ocean's summer mixed layer sat at its 10 m floor and 60–70°N seasons reached ~20 K (3.7 → 23.9 °C); with it they halved. It also made the shallow mixed layers a heat sink (49 W/m² below them at a 12 °C start), cured by counting the absorbed sunlight in the mixing's energy (#74). |

With two, upwelling brought the 4 °C abyss to the surface and the tropics cooled to 14–21 °C, and the TOA imbalance reached +8 to +24 W/m². The third layer costs ~1.5–2 ms per model hour at n = 6 (measured: coupled 44.7 against air 39.7, #82). A fourth, seasonal-thermocline layer (150 m, tried on the owner's request, n = 4, 4 years) cut the subtropical mixing's downward heat pump from 15.4 to 1.6 W/m² and brought the TOA to ~0 within 3–4 years from any thermocline start, but left the layer below it stagnant (500 m everywhere, at its starting temperature) and the equator warming to 30 °C; it was declined (#75). |

Raising it to 4 or 10 delayed but did not prevent the temperature-dependent-density ocean's blow-up (#64); removing it blew up sooner. Air results bit-identical. |
| 73 | 2026-10-27 | Crossings' kinetic energy as heat | Bug fix under the standing rule that every kinetic-energy change is booked as the air's heat (v1 §18 #36): air crossing an interface mixes its momentum into the layer it joins (Layers.exchange, upwind), which removes kinetic energy that was not booked. The exchange now returns that loss per edge, in the energy measure's own form, and it warms the column like the interfacial stress's (#39). Coupled n = 4, days 20–25: total energy change − TOA −0.059 → −0.0059 W/m² (left: the air's ∇⁴ viscosity and transport −0.0039, the ocean's unbooked mechanical dissipation −0.0015). The aquaplanet's own residual −0.114 → −0.038 W/m² (left: its surface drag, not yet booked). Changes air-only results (aqua and held_suarez hashes; rce unchanged); default suite 26/26. |
| 74 | 2026-10-27 | Sunlight in the mixed layer's energy | Agreed with the owner: the Kraus–Turner mixing counts the sunlight the mixed layer absorbs by where it is absorbed. Heat taken up at depth z costs (h/2 − z) per unit buoyancy to mix through a layer of depth h, against h/2 at the surface, so the sunlight's stabilizing flux is B_sun·[1 + f(h) − (2/h)∫₀ʰ f dz] (0.57 at 13 m, 0.67 at 40 m) instead of B_sun·[1 − f(h)] (0.76, 0.93); the shoaling depth, where wind stirring balances it, is found by bisection. With it, a sign slip in the Gaspar (1988) energy was corrected: heating now costs all of its work (h·B) and cooling yields the share n of its convection (n·h·|B|), where the code had charged heating n·h·B and credited cooling in full. Coupled n = 4, year 3: tropical (0–5°) mixed layer 11–18 → 74–81 m, SST 17.3–18.0 → 27.9–28.0 °C, TOA +23 to +30 → +7 to +12 W/m², sunlight below the mixed layer 49 → 22 W/m² (equator 64 → 3), the middle layer warming ~0.45 → ~0.25 K/yr, 60–70°N seasons 3.7–12.9 °C. The depth weighting alone: SST 25.6–25.9 °C, TOA +12 to +16, mid-latitude winter mixed layers 230–340 m; the sign alone: no gain (SST 17–18 °C, TOA +23 to +30). Energy: total change − TOA −0.009 W/m² (the ocean's unbooked mechanical energy −0.005 of it). |
| 75 | 2026-10-27 | Ocean warm start, three layers kept | Agreed with the owner: the ocean and the air both stay at three layers, and the ocean's middle layer starts at 20 °C (T_thermo 293.15 K, no warmer than the surface above it; the abyss at 4 °C) instead of 12 °C. Its slow drift is spin-up: the subtropics' daily mixing leaves warm water in the 600 m layer and takes back cooler water (≈ 18 of its 22 W/m² gain at a 12 °C start), sunlight below thin summer mixed layers adds 22 W/m² that winter convection mostly returns, and the uptake falls ≈ 1.9 W/m² per K as the layer warms. A seasonal-thermocline fourth layer, which would remove the pump at its source, was declined. Coupled n = 4, 4-year starts at 12/16/20/22/24/26 °C: year-4 TOA +10.2/+5.8/+3.1/+2.2/+1.5/+0.9 W/m², equatorial SST 27.4–28.0 (cooling) … 29.1–29.3 (steady at 20 °C) … 30.1–30.4 °C (warming); the equatorial middle layer keeps its starting temperature for years. 20 years from 20 °C (end-of-year values): TOA +4.1 → +0.3 W/m², middle layer 14.1 → 15.3 °C (0.04 K/yr at the end), equatorial SST 28.4 → 29.9 °C, abyss 3.96 → 3.77 °C, 60–70°N winter 3.4 °C; from 16 °C it follows ~2 K behind (TOA +2.4 at year 20). Tests take their coupled state from a cached spin-up. |
| 76 | 2026-10-27 | Ocean salinity (the freezing rule) | Agreed with the owner (salinity chosen over a halocline threshold or a lake-style density): every ocean layer carries a salinity, starting at 35 psu, moved with the water (FCT transport and the mixing's exchange). Rain, evaporation and the sea ice's growth and melt freshen or salt the mixed layer every air step as a virtual salt flux at S_ref = 35 psu; salt closes to 1e-16 of that flux over 30 days. Convection and Kraus–Turner mixing use the buoyancy b/g = α θ + α2 θ²/2 − β S (θ in °C; α 0.52e-4/K, α2 1.0e-5/K², β 7.6e-4/psu: seawater's expansion falling toward freezing), including the fresh water's buoyancy flux; the layers' g' stays fixed (#64). Coupled n = 4, 4 years: SSS 34.6 psu under the ITCZ, 35.1 in the subtropics, 34.95 at 60–70°N; the realistic and a constant α (2e-4) give the same climate within 0.3 K. No sea ice forms: polar columns convect to the bottom each winter, so the polar surface stays at the deep water's temperature (3.4 °C with the abyss at 4 °C; 0.2 °C, with traces of ice, from a 0.5 °C abyss; −0.8 °C and none from −1 °C with seawater's freezing point −1.9 °C), and a halocline cannot build. The freezing point stays the air physics' 0 °C. |
| 77 | 2026-10-27 | Abyss starts at freezing | Agreed with the owner: the abyss starts at 0 °C (T_deep 273.15 K, the air physics' freezing point) instead of 4 °C, the deep counterpart of #75: polar winters would cool it there over centuries (it lost ~0.01 K/yr from 4 °C), and while it is warmer every polar column convects to the bottom and no ice can form. Coupled n = 4, 20 years from 0 °C (from 0.5 °C in brackets): sea ice from year 1 (year 4), its winter cover 7.7–8.3% of the planet from year 5 (7.4–7.9% from year 13), 70–90° winter ice 1.4–1.7 m and still thickening ~0.05 m/yr (1.4 m); the ice's melt and growth cycle builds a polar halocline, SSS 70–90° 35.0 → 32.4 psu (32.5); 70–90° summer SST 11.8 → 1.8 °C (11.9 → 2.4); TOA (annual mean) +8.5 → +4.0 W/m² (+9.1 → +3.5), the middle layer still warming 0.04 K/yr and equatorial SST 27.9 → 29.4 °C; the abyss stays at 0.00 °C (0.46 °C); ocean currents ≤ 0.38 m/s. |
| 78 | 2026-10-27 | Ocean Kelvin wave gate | Agreed with the owner: WAVES's ocean Kelvin wave (n = 6, the ocean's default fixed-g' layers with their friction and smoothing, no air; a linear first-baroclinic-mode bump tracked along the equator from day 5 to 25) passes within 2% of c1 from the layered eigenproblem (3.073 m/s). Measured 3.051 m/s (−0.73%); without friction and smoothing −0.01%; at n = 5 −2.9% (the 366 km equatorial deformation radius is ~1.5 cells there), hence n = 6. |
| 79 | 2026-10-27 | Ocean basin preset | Agreed with the owner: a `basin` preset (the coupled world with one thin pole-to-pole wall at 180°), loadable in the viewer and by tests. A wall is a set of edges the ocean cannot cross — its velocity and flux are zeroed there after every kernel that updates them, fast substeps included (Layers' `wall` mask; Ocean's `walls`, longitudes in degrees); there are no land cells, so the air still sees open water, and the basin stays closed except through the single cell at each pole. Why: the open aquaplanet's ocean has no coasts, so no gyres or zonal pressure gradient — at n = 4 its heat transport was ±0.0–0.3 PW with no consistent direction and its thermocline flat. With the wall (15 years from the coupled spin-up, last year measured): heat transport poleward at every latitude, 0.52 / 0.35 PW at 20°S / N and 0.56 / 0.55 PW at 30° (0.47–0.83 PW with the lowest air interface at 315 K); the equatorial thermocline base 723 m deep in the west and 683 m in the east (657 m at 315 K); equatorial SST 30.5 °C in the west and 26.9 °C at the east coast; the western side 0.7 K warmer than the interior at 15–40°N; the equator still 0.7 K warmer than 4–8° in the east (no equatorial dip). Published idealized coupled studies use the same thin ridge (e.g. Ferreira, Marshall & Rose 2010). Uncoupled and `coupled` worlds bit-identical. The spin-up cache key now ignores line endings. |
| 80 | 2026-10-27 | Coupled CLIMATE targets | Agreed with the owner: CLIMATE coupled runs on `basin` (n = 4, year 21 after the cached spin-up) with the targets in §7. Changed from the slab's CLIMATE: \|TOA\| < 1 becomes the air's own balance against the ocean's and ice's heat uptake (the deep ocean still takes ~3.6–4 W/m² after 20 years, falling ~0.03 W/m² a year); the ITCZ ratio > 2 becomes > 1.3 (coupled ×1.44–1.50 over four years: the equator stays warm, with no upwelled dip). Added: equator − pole 30–60 K and 60°N seasons > 3 K (#52, deferred to the ocean), the ocean's heat transport, thermocline tilt, east–west SST contrast and sea-ice edge, thresholds at about a third of the values measured over years 21–23 (heat transport 0.36–0.73 PW at 20–30°, tilt 29–31 m, contrast 0.90–1.03 K, edge 58–65°); the equatorial dip is reported only. The ocean's heat transport is summed inside the step's graph (World.track_ocean_heat_transport), keeping the measured year at graph speed. First result: 289.67 K; TOA +3.61, uptake +3.62 W/m² (air −0.00); rain 2.26 mm/day, ITCZ ×1.44, 40–60° ×1.35; equator − pole 36.2 K; 60°N seasons 8.4 K; heat transport −0.51/−0.38/+0.37/+0.31 PW at 30S/20S/20N/30N; tilt +32 m; contrast +1.12 K; ice edge 58–64°; dip −0.62 K. |

| 83 | 2026-10-28 | Nearly empty air layers take their neighbours' wind | Agreed with the owner (the goal: one model for any n, no per-resolution tuning): where the thinner of an edge's two cells holds less than 100 kg/m² (~1 hPa) of an air layer, that layer's wind there is blended toward the mass-weighted wind of the layers above and below, by (100 − h)/(100 − 10), all the way at the 10 kg/m² floor; the momentum it gains comes from those neighbours (column momentum exact) and the kinetic energy removed warms the air (#73), inside the crossings' exchange kernel (no extra launch). Why: the slab `aqua` blew up at n = 6 (day 131 on V2-2b's code, day 151 on V2-3's; never seen at n = 4–5, and PERF ran n = 6 only a day). A middle-layer cell (53.5°S, 130°E) emptied to its floor and its edges' winds ran away cell by cell (±150 → ±2,400 m/s in ~0.03 day); the wind budget showed the kinetic-energy gradient alone growing (32 → 1,708 → 16,668 m/s per hour) while the Coriolis term stayed ~5 — a nearly empty layer has no mass to build the pressure that resists it. Tried and not adopted: half the step (blew up sooner, day 149), a floor of 100 kg/m² (151), the Coriolis thin cap at 1× (176), the interfacial stress at c = 3e-3 kg/m²/s (stable but storms weakened: B4 0.45 → 0.27, B9 missed again), a thin-layer stress (169), and a cell/vertex kinetic-energy blend (151–160, so not the classic Hollingsworth mode). With the rule, one setting: `aqua` stable for 2 years at n = 4, 5 and 6 and 6 months at n = 7 (max |u| 43–79 m/s); CLIMATE, WEATHER and the coupled tests unchanged to the printed digits (no layer gets that thin there); suite 36/36, PERF air 39.5 ms, coupled 44.8 ms. Held–Suarez and short runs from rest change slightly (layers briefly that thin in the first hours). The stage close adds a 2-year `aqua` run at n = 6. |
| 84 | 2026-10-31 | Land (V2-4a) | Agreed with the owner. **Design:** land is a mask from the preset, any shape; ocean walled off at its coasts; ground heat capacity 2e6 J/m²/K; albedo 0.30 dry to 0.18 wet; v1's soil bucket (capacity 150 kg/m² + 0.5 per m of height; full evaporation above 75%); snow on ground below freezing; runoff to the nearest coast until rivers (V2-4b); θ* stays 305 K. **Gates:** GYRE, CONSERVE land, CLIMATE land and RIDGE as in §7; PERF land ≤ 50 ms per model hour at n = 6. **Changed gates, CLIMATE land only:** ITCZ measured over the sea, since the land mass's own dry equator is reported separately; equator − pole reported, not gated at 30–60 K (28.0 K): it is the response of this planet, not an error (below), and `continent`'s outline was not moved to pass. **Polar warmth:** with ice held and the ocean walled at 70°, land reaching 70° rather than 50° brings +20.5 W/m² more air heat into the northern cap (135.5 against 115.0, 11 months of 12; about 0.3 PW), amplified about 2.6× by ice loss when the ice is free; thick-ice restarts return in 3–5 years, so no bistability. n = 4; the circulation behind it is not separated from storms. **Known shortfalls, open:** (1) rain shadow backwards (RIDGE ×0.57, target > 2×, reported). (2) Equatorial land is dry: 0.35–0.41 mm/day against ~2.8 over the sea, its soil ~9% full, its air sinking, with middle-layer vapor import (+0.97 mm/day) carried out again by the bottom layer (−0.94). Tested, none fixes it (rain, last year of a 4–6 year run from the spin-up, soil free unless stated): soil held full 1.9; albedo 0.12 / 0.06 with soil free 0.1–0.3 / 0.3–0.5, held full 2.8–3.0 / 4.3; flat land 0.02 (spun up); the parcel also tested at the middle layer's mean pressure, everywhere 0.00 with sea rain 6–7 mm/day and global rain above 3, land only 0.58 and falling; rising air condensing at the interface 0.32. A bottom layer of only 50–125 hPa at 0–10° (thick only poleward) gives no reason to expect the hybrid lower layer (plan §3) to fix this. Why a wet state cannot sustain itself is not known; V2-4b's rivers start on this climate. |
| 85 | 2026-10-31 | Surface water (V2-4b Step 1) | Agreed with the owner: **v1's flow rule is not used**, since it moved a fixed share of the head per tick whatever the cell size, so a flow's speed changed with n. A flow now crosses cells at a set speed of 1 m/s (an assumption, measured 0.998–1.022 at n = 4–8): through an edge of length l, ¾ U l min(head, depth), at most 1/12 of the head as v1, on an hourly routing step (the ocean's, or the air's step without an ocean). Water surface b + pond/ρ_w + ice/ρ_ice. Rain and melt reach a pond (kg/m²) that covers pond/50 kg/m² of the cell at the sea's albedo and evaporates at the full rate; it soaks in at v1's 5 mm/hour × (1 − soil/capacity), not on ground below freezing, where it freezes, warming the ground; what the soil cannot hold returns to the pond. Seepage is v1's Darcy rule, K = 2e4 m²/hour, with the table (soil/capacity)·b, limited to 1/12 of what levels two tables. Scaling it ×0, ×0.01 and ×1 leaves land rain at 0.72–0.77 mm/day (runs with the soaking rate then wrong). What reaches a sea cell waits at its mouth for the ocean's fresh water, replacing #84's jump to the nearest coast; it carries no heat (my estimate: 0.7 W/m² of land left out). A new `relief` preset keeps `continent` comparable. Gates: WATER (§7). Found on the way: a rounding-negative pond gave NaN in the limiter, which froze `continent` to 255.9 K; the soaking rate was first entered 1000× too small, which WATER could not see, so it now checks the rate itself. |
| 86 | 2026-10-31 | Ice sheets (V2-4b Step 2) | Agreed with the owner: Glen's shallow-ice flow, n = 3, without sliding, A = 1e-16 Pa⁻³/yr everywhere (no temperature dependence; an assumption). Through an edge, Γ H⁵ \|∇s\|² (head/de) le, H the donor's thickness, \|∇s\|² from the TRiSK kinetic-energy rule, at most 1/12 of the head; s = b + ice/ρ_ice. **Two departures from the plan as first put:** it runs on the hourly routing step, not a step of its own, since a separate step needs more captured graphs and steps from 1 to 40 years give the same dome to 4 mm; and ice reaching the coast becomes that sea cell's sea ice rather than direct fresh water, so that it melts by the existing column physics, taking its latent heat from the sea, with energy and salt closing by the existing rules. Sea ice stays column-only, so calved ice does not drift. Snow remains the land's ice store (no separate snowpack or compaction). The air does not feel the ice's height: terrain is fixed. Threshold kept at 10% as measured (height −3.8%, volume −6.3% at n = 6; volume converges slowly). `relief` grows no ice sheet (25 cm), so the flow has no effect yet on the climates tested; open shortfall. |
| 87 | 2026-11-01 | Snow and albedos by surface (V2-4b) | Agreed with the owner. **A bug of mine fixed:** snow or rain was chosen by the ground's temperature, so over thick ice (ground about 273 K, surface about 260 K) a sheet got rain, and snow's latent heat melted it from below; a started 2 km sheet lost about 260 kg/m² a year. Where ice covers the ground (thicker than ice_cover), the skin now decides, the freezing's heat goes to the bottom air, and no water soaks in. **Albedos by surface, after a fixed 0.8 on land ice was first adopted and then dropped:** it left ice through the year on 57% of `continent` by year 12, still spreading. A snow store (the part of the land's ice that fell as snow, melting first; state `snow`) covers snow/30 kg/m² of the ice. Snow 0.80 cold to 0.55 at freezing air; bare glacier ice 0.35; sea ice 0.70 to 0.50, over 10 K below freezing, by the air touching the ground. These values are assumptions within observed ranges. Sea ice's 0.50 was chosen after 0.45 took CLIMATE aqua to 291.19 K against its 291 K limit, so it is partly fitted to that gate. **Gate changed:** the agreed gate (polar land gains ice from nothing) cannot pass, since land starting bare keeps melting out; replaced by a started 2 km sheet losing ≤ 50 kg/m² a year, growth from nothing reported. Open shortfall: the air does not feel the ice's height, which may be what stops a sheet growing from nothing. |

The fix is a firn with its own stored cold, as real physics in V2-6. |

Layer placement helps up to ×2 (380/320 K). With Held–Suarez forcing, today's 380/305 K layers reach 9.2e4 kg/s², so the core can carry it. **Confirmed:** weakening surface heat exchange strengthens eddies (dry h_sens 10 → 1: ×3.3; steady sun ×4.8; moist 10 → 3: ×2). v1's form keeps 10 W/m²/K at calm. **Behind switches, off:** the bulk form with gusts (`bulk_c`, little effect alone), stability (`bulk_z`, moist ×2 but poleward, dry worse), a thin surface layer in series (`bulk_w`, up to ×1.9 moist). With Held–Suarez or the plain plume the storms are stronger, but the full-depth plume makes the tropics right (mass flux 276 against 87 kg/m²/day at 8°, middle θ 329 against 321 K) and weakens mid-latitude eddies ×1.8 without changing mid-latitude plumes, cause untested; entrainment (`plume_ent`, off) did not restore them. **Paused by the owner the same day:** winds stay 3–5× weak, reported, not pass or fail; regional rain and currents are provisional; to return with V2-6's boundary layer and radiation. **Lightning takes no global target (owner):** a flash rate from each storm's own physics, with its constant from published storm-level measurements, never fitted to the model's global rate; the global rate and land-to-ocean ratio are reported against Earth's, and should nearly match once winds and rain are fixed. |

Real tropical thermoclines are cooled from below by the overturning's upwelling (~30 Sv over the ocean, w ≈ 1e-7 m/s; ρc w ΔT ≈ 12 W/m² across a 29 K step) and diapycnal mixing (κ 1e-5–1e-4 m²/s: 0.6–6 W/m² here), the same size as the layer's residual warming. Next: deep-water formation and crossing at the layers' interfaces (step 3). |

Interior mixing (`kappa_d`, m²/s; Munk 1966): the diffusion of density across an interface carried in the fixed classes as the lower layer's water rising, w = 2κ/(h_k + h_k+1). **Measured (trials):** on the three-layer ocean from #158's equilibrium, κ_d 1e-4 thickened layer 1 (632 → 693 m) and warmed the abyss until the air went NaN in the fourth cycle; κ_d 1e-5 with formation ran stably, but the shallow seas cut off from the open ocean (#97) went to −51 … +256 psu and 45–90 °C bottom water under the virtual salt flux (fixed by #165). Its wind (60 days from 1 January, zonal means over the sea): eastward stress +0.059 N/m² at 45°S (bottom wind +4.2 m/s), +0.005 at 55°S (+0.8), easterly −0.023 at 65°S (−1.4); the real westerly stress peaks ~0.15–0.2 N/m² at 50–55°S. So the chain is a loop: westerlies too far north → no stress over the Drake Passage latitudes → weak circumpolar current, flat layers → warm water south to Antarctica, no sea ice, warm moist overcast polar air (#176, #177) → a weak 45–65°S gradient → westerlies too far north. Atmospheric 500 hPa temperature against NCEP is within 3 K over Antarctica and the Arctic (the polar error is below 850 hPa, over the warm sea) and 4–5 K cold in the tropics and subtropics. | 218 | 2026-11-16 | The land column: soil layers under the ground's skin (`soil_dz`, off; V2-6e, land column) | **Built:** with `soil_dz` (a list of layer thicknesses, m; `constants.SOIL_DZ` 0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 0.8, 1.6, 3.2, to 6.3 m, about three annual damping depths) each land cell has a column of soil layers under the skin, their temperatures `World.oTg` (K × N, in the save), in place of #118's single deep ground (force-restore, c_deep, which must be 0 with it). Heat is conducted between neighbouring layers across their centres, the skin counted as thick as its capacity in soil (c_land / c_soil, 6 cm on earth), with none through the bottom; the soil is moist mineral soil, volumetric heat capacity `c_soil` 2.0e6 J/m³/K and conductivity `k_soil` 1.0 W/m/K (Hillel 1982, Oke 1987; damping depths 2.2 m for the year, 0.12 m for the day). Stepped implicitly (backward Euler, a tridiagonal solve per column, `land_column` in ocean.cu), stable at any layer thickness, its heat kept to rounding; the ocean's heat ledger counts it. | 235 | 2026-11-20 | V2-7 Mars step 2 (owner: 'Continue with step 2'; 'Keep improving tables'; 'work on a different top layer radiation method'): radiation tables down to ~100 K, Mars's own longwave set, and LONGWAVE gains Mars's mean day and winter pole | **Built:** line strengths judged at 100, 180 and 320 K and the partition sums from 70 K (lbl.set_cold); tables 100–340 K, Planck 90–350 K, shortwave 90–330 K; each planet's gravity in the line-by-line and the fit; the grouping's features at the same shares of a thin air's surface pressure; empty g-points 0 (not 0/0). **Mars's own set** (`tables='mars'` in constants.MARS; radiation.TABLES; files lw/sw/cia_ktables_mars.npz): one set fitted on Earth's stratosphere and Mars's lower air together failed both (they share pressures, 50–100 K and ~2000× the CO₂ apart), so Mars is grouped and fitted on three Mars columns alone (map_mars, map_mars_cold, map_mars_inv). 138 g-points [4, 4, 22, 38, 30, 8, 8, 8, 8, 8], within the kernel's 144. Mars's shortwave unfitted: on Mars's columns alone the fit ran away (factors e^±40) and doubled the flux error (2.1 against 1.1 W/m², heating 0.38 K/day). **The top layer** (the winter pole's 3.7–13.7 Pa, 0.243 K/day = 2.4% over with 120 g-points and a pressure-only fit): the tables match exact per-layer group means to 0.015 K/day and line-by-line is converged (a 4× finer step changes it ≤ 0.001 K/day), so the error is the grouping's. Above ~400 Pa a line's width is set by molecular speed, not collisions, so a wing thick below is thin at the top: wavenumbers grouped together lower down part there, and the group's mean optical depth puts the top's opacity in the gaps between line centres, where it absorbs the warmer radiation from below; the error depends on the column below, so no pressure–temperature factor can mend it (40 random training columns with such a factor: 0.52 K/day). Failed on the way: weights on the upper air (to 0.47), lower feature floors 1e-6/1e-8 (0.47), weighting or halving-and-splitting on top features (lower levels to 0.6), more g-points (144, 140, 176 unfitted: 0.26–0.48), a bottom split (−0.17 → −0.13 unfitted). **Adopted: a top split** (other_air.top_split): after the k-means, in each of CO₂'s three bands (500–630, 630–700, 700–820 cm⁻¹), the two g-points whose top two sub-levels the grouping misjudges most on the map columns are split four ways by the top layer's own optical depth (+18 g-points); unfitted the winter pole's top goes −0.24 → +0.003 K/day. **And a temperature term in the fit** (make_ktables.T_FIT = (170 K, 50 K), Mars's set only): the grouping over-cools warm air in the band wings by a few per cent (unfitted −0.2 K/day through the mean column, −1.1 in map_mars), local in temperature, so each g-point's log factor gains a term linear in (T − 170 K)/50 K on the same pressure nodes (applied on the tables' temperatures, no cost in the model); without it the fit overshot the mean column's bottom sub-level (−6.28 against −6.485 K/day). **LONGWAVE** (thresholds unchanged): Mars's mean day (610 Pa, 140–215 K) OLR 111.29 against 111.28 W/m², surface down −0.16%, worst sub-level 0.052 K/day; winter pole (700 Pa, 127–165 K) OLR 28.51 against 28.52, surface down +0.87%, top sub-level −10.082 against −10.066 K/day (0.016), worst 0.068; other air OLR 0.09%, surface down 0.87%, sub-levels over both limits 0; kernels 9.8e-7. Earth's columns, tables and presets unchanged (earth7, coupled, earth4, w2 bit-identical in 64-bit). The wide set's cache intermediates (map_wide, swmap_wide, lw_wide, sw_wide in cache/ktables) were overwritten by the mixed trial and need its pipeline rerun only if it is rebuilt. **So:** Mars at n = 4 runs on its tables (20 sols checked); on them the polar air cools below 150 K with nothing to stop it at CO₂'s frost point, so the long run breaks sooner (sol 105 against 156): CO₂ frost (step 4) comes before the surface maps (step 3). |

So the storms grow on the 305 and 315 K slopes at 35–40°S and their waves carry momentum out of 45–65°S; the warm Southern Ocean (#178) is downstream of this. **Next (proposed):** an interface in the extratropical lower troposphere (the cloud layer split by θ, e.g. at 295 K, whose observed surface spans 999 hPa at 22.5°S to 381 at 77.5°S) with the cumulus code taking the first layer with air above the cumulus layer as its cloud layer (the tropics' cloud layer would hold no air below 295 K). |

| 272 | 2026-11-26 | V2-7 the squeezed layer's runaway traced: TRiSK's PV flux gives a thin edge between thick vertices a fraction of its Coriolis force (owner: 'Trace thin layer') | none (measurement) | **The form** (lay_slow, swe_vertex): an edge's Coriolis term is Σ w F_e′ (q_e + q_e′)/2 with F_e′ = h_e′ u_e′ its neighbours' fluxes (h_e′ capped at `thin` = 2 × the edge's own, #38) and q = (ζ + f)/h_v, h_v the kite-weighted thickness of the vertex's three cells. It equals f × the tangential wind only where the edges' and vertices' thicknesses agree. **Measured** (corio.py, one plain tick, edges at 30–70° with tangential wind > 10 m/s; the ratio of the term to f v_t, binned by h_e/h_v): n = 6 (#270's caught state) 305–315 K layer 0.17 for h_e/h_v < 0.2 (1,015 edges), 0.42 for 0.2–0.5 (2,374), 1.01 for 0.5–2 (11,768), 2.08 above 2 (1,112, the cap); the other free layers alike (0.16 / 0.40–0.47 / 1.0 / 2.0); the #38 cap almost never binds (capped/uncapped 1.00) except in the cloud layer's thinnest (0.59). The runaway edge (315–330 K, −121.6 m/s at 47°S): h_e 20.9 hPa against h_v 199 hPa, its Coriolis term 0.37 of f v_t: the pressure force there is only part balanced, and the wind runs down it. **Against resolution:** mismatched edges (h_e/h_v < 0.5 or > 2) are 10% / 11% of the fast edges in the 315–330 / 305–315 K layers at n = 4 (am4n) and 17% / 28% at n = 6, where each faces ~4× the pressure gradient of the same relative noise (#270). **Reading:** the real Coriolis force is f × u whatever the layer's thickness; the discrete one follows the ratio of neighbouring thicknesses, which the condensation's grid-scale layer noise (#271) drives far from 1. TRiSK's energy neutrality holds for any vertex q with the symmetric (q_e + q_e′)/2 (Thuburn et al. 2009), so a vertex thickness consistent with the fluxes it multiplies (e.g. from its edges' h_e rather than its cells') keeps the energy exact while restoring the force; that would change PV's definition at the vertices. **Proposed next:** that change trialled at n = 6 (does it survive) and n = 4 (EARTH, WINDS, RAIN). |

(A first version with the writes inside lay_slow itself was not bit-identical off, the compiler fusing its arithmetic differently: so the separate copy.) **Run** (ib.py: n = 6 from #269's day-120 state under WOA23, every tick checked, the instrument on from \|u\|max 112, a ring of the last 40 ticks of layers 1–4): runs away at day 55 tick 167 as before (#273's ~55 days), layer 2 (330–380 K), the edge between 49.5°S 16.0°W and 49.2°S 17.6°W, −52.6 → −152.3 m/s in 40 ticks (450 s). **Budget over those 40 ticks** (m/s): −∇K −95.3, pressure (−∇(B − K) + π̄∇θ + fast −∇φ) −40.7, PV flux +12.1, ∇⁴ +12.3, drag/stress/vmix 0.0, exchange +1.0; total −101.0. −∇K is −0.6 to −5.9 every tick and grows with the wind; the pressure term is −1 to −5 while the layer is 35–70 hPa thick and changes sign (−1.3 to +2.2) once squeezed to 10–20 hPa; the PV flux stays ≈ f × the tangential wind (+0.8 → −0.3 per tick); ∇⁴ grows to +1 per tick, a fifth of −∇K. **The edge at the hit** (kloc.py): −152 m/s against its cells' other edges −61, +34, −61, −36, −32 and +4, −87, +97, −24, −66; it alone holds 3,920 of cell I's 5,692 J/kg and 3,927 of cell J's 7,574 J/kg (the cells' speeds 107 and 123 m/s, slower than the edge). Its own share is the same in both cells and cancels in the gradient; −∇K = −(K_J − K_I)/d_e comes from the neighbours (−7.0 m/s per tick). **Reading:** a grid-scale spike of one edge's normal wind, faster than the air in either cell. The C-grid's centred K gradient leaves out the edge's own wind, so the faster neighbourhood upstream keeps accelerating it past the upstream speed (an advective form would stop at it). Nothing in the thin layer resists: its pressure response scales with its mass, the PV flux is Coriolis on the tangential wind, and ∇⁴ (ν4 = U·d³/π⁴ with U = 15 m/s, a fixed damping rate ~U/d) loses to a growth rate ~\|u\|/d once the wind is several times U. The same signature as #83's nearly empty layers (the KE gradient growing, Coriolis small), here in a 10–60 hPa layer, which #83's 1 hPa blend does not reach. #272's Coriolis deficit is real but secondary. **Open:** a damping that scales with the flow (a biharmonic Smagorinsky viscosity, Griffies & Hallberg 2000, ν4 ∝ \|deformation\|·d⁴, as in MOM and MPAS-Ocean), or an advective-consistent kinetic energy at edges. |

The model's air has no shear-driven vertical mixing in the free troposphere (vmix 0 at the edge, #274). **Reading:** the physical process missing is the shear instability's momentum mixing between layers, which every GCM parameterises (a Richardson-number-dependent diffusivity, e.g. the free-atmosphere mixing of ECMWF's IFS or Louis 1979); it acts exactly on a thin fast layer between slower ones and is implicit in the vertical, so it has no ∇⁴'s step limit. Not built: put to the owner. |
