| 281 | 2026-11-27 | V2-7 Found: the summer desert's mixed layer is shallow because it is wet, fed by its own ground; deepening it past its cloud base has no physical basis (owner: 'Work on fix 1 for the dry mixed layer after this n=6 runaway problem') | **Vapor budget** (dml.py, tools/vapor_budget.py's VAPDIAG stages; #280's earth9 equilibrium, the June solstice + 10 days, 30 days; kg/m²/day; layers top → bottom). **Sahara** 18–30°N 15°W–30°E: mean masses 53/44/289/221/80/6/8/110/127 hPa, θ 655/436/338/322/313/313/313/309/308 K, vapor 0/0/0.1/2.0/3.8/5.6/5.9/9.7/10.4 g/kg (observed near the ground ~6–8 g/kg, θ ~315 K, the dry layer to ~500 hPa). The mixed layer's vapor: surface evaporation +3.51, sideways transport +1.42, the crossings −3.23, the plumes −8.57 with the shallow plume's return +6.21. Its exchanges before the plumes (air / vapor): entrainment +410 / +3.43 (from the 9.7 g/kg layer above), sent up above its cloud base −440 / −5.30, the night's return −229 / −2.83, the crossing +124 / +1.25, the dry reach +39 / +0.21. Large-scale rain comes from the 322 K layer (−1.32) and the layer over the mixed layer (−0.91). **Arabia** 15–30°N 38–56°E: evaporation +4.40, transport into the mixed layer +3.15, mixed layer 11.8 g/kg; the 322 K layer rains −3.54. **Sahel** 10–18°N: evaporation +4.50, transport +2.21, 12.1 g/kg. **The soil** (dsoil.py, every 15 days from the equinox): the Sahara's 14.8 kg/m² (of 150) at the equinox dries to 10.9 by day 30, then the summer rain fills it to 74.3 by day 120, with ponds up to 10.8; Arabia holds 58–118 all season with ponds of 20–27 kg/m² in spring. **So:** the mixed layer stops at its cloud base, where #124 sends its air up (−440 kg/m²/day), because it is moist (10.4 g/kg) and cool (308 K); its vapor comes mostly from the ground, which the summer's rain wets. A dry mixed layer deepening past its cloud base would be cloud, not dry air: #208's prescription does not hold, and nothing was built. The desert's dryness needs the summer rain's first source cut (the 322 K layer's imported vapor, #243) or the wet ground's evaporation corrected. No code or threshold changed. |
| 282 | 2026-11-27 | V2-7 Found: the desert's first rain is vapor pumped up through the emptied layers by the crossings' churn (owner: 'The first rain') | **May** (dml.py, #280's earth9 equilibrium + 40 days, 30 days; kg/m²/day): the Sahara's mixed layer is deep (240 hPa) and drier (4.6 g/kg, 307.8 K), evaporating 0.54; the large-scale rain falls from the 315–330 K layer (−0.44, Arabia −1.28) and the cloud layer (−0.25, −0.98), the 315–330 K layer's vapor coming from the crossings (+0.44, Arabia +1.46), not from the winds (+0.04, −0.36). **The crossings by interface and rule** (dxc.py, a scratch air.cu recording each interface's mass and vapor moved and the rule that moved it; same 30 days): the vapor goes net up through every interface between the cloud layer and the 315–330 K layer (Sahara +0.93/+0.71/+0.63/+0.44, Arabia +1.43/+1.52/+1.41/+1.50 across the cumulus top, 295, 305 and 315 K edges), carried by a churn of whole thin layers: the two emptied layers between 295 K and the cumulus top (9–20 hPa, θ 313.0/312.9 against edges cu_edges holds 0.5 K apart just above the cumulus top) are pushed up whole as warmer than their top edge (Sahara up to +754 kg/m²/day of air, +2.32 of vapor across the 305 K edge; Arabia +556, +2.60) and pushed back down as colder than their bottom edge (−733, −1.31; −483, −0.84), with radiative edge crossings on top (the cloud layer heated at its top +356 kg/m²/day, the 305–315 K layer cooled at its bottom −594). Up and down nearly cancel in air but not in vapor: the air going up is the moist air from below, the air coming down the drier air from above, so the churn diffuses vapor up through the stable 310–320 K layer over the boundary layer. Across the 315 K edge (Arabia +1.50: re-sorted whole +0.83, heated at the edge +1.12, cooled −0.46) it supplies the 315–330 K layer's whole rain. No code or threshold changed. |

| 285 | 2026-11-27 | V2-7 Found: the 315–330 K layer's desert rain is saturated ascent, a moist 305–315 K layer's own latent heat lifting it across 315 K (owner: 'Yes, trace it') | **Where it forms** (lsr.py; #283's scratch copy with cu_hold off, the equilibrium + 40 days, 30 days of May; VAPDIAG's cloud rows, binned by the layer's total water against its capacity): the 315–330 K layer holds on average 0.52 (Sahara) and 0.44 (Arabia) of its capacity, but its cloud forms only where it is over it: over 1.1 in 3.6% (Sahara) and 7.3% (Arabia) of the cell-ticks, which form 0.29 of 0.45 and 1.23 of 1.29 kg/m²/day; below 0.8 nothing forms. **The events** (lsev.py, 4295 cell-ticks over 1.1 in 50 Sahara and Arabia cells, medians): the layer is not thinned (243 hPa before and after) nor cooled (θ 321.3 both); its vapor goes 1.66 → 1.97 g/kg within the tick against a capacity of 1.58 (its typical 0.40 of 1.03), carried in across the 315 K edge (+53 kg/m² that tick, up in 86% of events), by the 305–315 K layer heated at its top edge (63%; whole as warmer than that edge, 30%). That layer is thin and moist there (41 hPa, 4.64 g/kg) and heated by its own condensation, which the crossing takes as heating at its top edge: saturated air lifted by its latent heat, carrying its vapor up into a layer that saturates in turn.
